### plan-r3.md

# Plan: database deletion removes only its own preferred search-index sidecar

## Goal

`unlink_database_files` must not destroy a valid search-index sidecar that belongs to another
database. Today it unlinks the preferred `.ecsi` sidecar before it learns whether the primary
leaf it is about to delete is still the database the caller resolved. The fix gives the preferred
sidecar the same kind of provenance check the legacy sidecar already has. The check reads the
sidecar's recorded database object through the retained parent, and the removal is bound to the
identity of that same opened file.

## MANDATE

Verbatim from `tasks/findings.md`, `f-20260912-09`:

> ### `unlink_database_files` removes the preferred sidecar before it checks the primary leaf, so a database replaced after the caller's probe loses the replacement's index
>
> * **Where:** `src-tauri/src/db/mod.rs:2257-2282` (`unlink_database_files`: `remove_optional_regular_at(preferred)`, then the legacy sidecar, then the primary's `statat` identity check, then `unlinkat`), the caller `delete_database_blocking` (`:2188-2215`, which probes through `database_identity_expected` first), and `d-20260831-24` (sidecar first, then legacy, then primary — the recorded order).
> * **Defect:** the caller's probe and the function's own primary check both happen at instants; between the probe and `remove_optional_regular_at`, or between a moved-up `statat` and that removal, an external rename can install database B and B's `.ecsi` at the same pathname. The preferred sidecar removed is then B's, and only afterwards does the primary check return `Conflict`. Sidecars are regenerable, so nothing is lost permanently, but a valid index is destroyed for a database the user did not ask to touch. Moving the `statat` before the sidecar removal narrows the window without closing it (measured in plan review, 2026-09-12: four lenses independently described the same post-check swap).
> * **Open question:** what closes the window — removing sidecars only after the primary has been unlinked (`d-20260831-24` chose sidecar-first so a failed primary unlink never leaves an orphaned index; the inverse leaves a sidecar whose provenance no longer matches, which the loader already rejects), or a sidecar identity/provenance check through the retained parent immediately before each `unlinkat` (the legacy path already does `legacy_sidecar_matches`)?
> * **Why it matters:** the deletion path's contract is "delete exactly the database the capability names"; an index for a different database is collateral.

## Threat model and non-goals

* **In scope: accidental concurrency.** Another program, such as a sync client, a backup restore
  or a file manager, renames a different database and its sidecar onto the same pathname
  while a deletion is running. The window that matters runs from the caller's identity probe
  through the exclusive-lock wait and the mapping-gate drain to the sidecar unlink. It can last
  as long as those waits take.
* **Out of scope: a deliberate adversary.** An adversary could race the single `statat`→`unlinkat`
  instant inside `remove_entry_at` on POSIX. POSIX has no pathname compare-and-unlink. The
  primary leaf and the legacy sidecar carry the same residual today, and
  `search_index.rs:914-919` already records it as a bounded known limit for legacy promotion.
  Windows deletes through the handle whose identity it checked
  (`infra/fs.rs:3637-3683`, `FILE_DISPOSITION_POSIX_SEMANTICS` per `d-20260919-10`), so Windows
  has no such residual.
* Frozen with the MANDATE.

## Traced premises

Read at `BASE` `e508f755`:

* P1 — `src-tauri/src/db/mod.rs:2665-2723` `delete_database_blocking`: `resolve_database(..,
  DatabaseMutate)` → `identity_from_probe` → `IndexSource::from_database_identity` → inside
  `delete_exclusive_cancellable`, `begin_preferred_replace` then `unlink_database_files(&target,
  &expected_source)`. `expected_source.object` is the probed database object.
* P2 — `src-tauri/src/db/mod.rs:2756-2925` `unlink_database_files` (the finding's line numbers
  have moved since it was filed). It snapshots the `-wal`/`-shm` identities, then runs
  `entry_identity_at(preferred)` followed by `remove_sidecar`. `remove_sidecar` calls
  `remove_entry_at(parent, leaf, identity, false)`. Then the legacy sidecar goes through
  `legacy_sidecar_matches`, then the primary goes through `remove_entry_at(target.identity())`,
  and a `Conflict` there becomes `Conflict("database changed before deletion")`. The SQLite
  sidecars are removed last. **The preferred sidecar's removal checks only that its
  `(dev, ino)` has not changed since a `statat` taken a moment earlier. Nothing ties that file
  to the database being deleted.**
* P3 — `src-tauri/src/db/mod.rs:2927-2949` `legacy_sidecar_matches` uses
  `search_index::probe_legacy_index_sidecar_at` (`search_index.rs:880-905`). That function
  opens the file through the retained parent, takes `opened_file_identity`, maps and fully
  validates the archive (`MmapSearchIndex::open_file`, which walks every chunk), compares the
  full `IndexSource`, and drops the mapping. A `Malformed`, `NotFound`, `Reparse` or `WrongKind`
  result means keep the file. `MappedFile` or `Other` propagates the error.
* P4 — `src-tauri/src/db/search_index.rs:165-171` `IndexSource { object, revision,
  database_length, database_modified_nanos }`. `object` is the database's `(dev, ino)` or, on
  Windows, its volume and file index. Every archive records the source it was built for.
* P5 — `src-tauri/src/db/search_index.rs:73-104` `verify_header` and `:620-645`
  (`open_file_inner`). The magic, version, `source_len` ≤ `MAX_SOURCE_BYTES` (1 MiB), alignment
  and rkyv decode of the source all run before any chunk is touched. `HEADER_SIZE` is 32 and
  `ARCHIVE_ALIGNMENT` is 16 (`:30-33`).
* P6 — `src-tauri/src/db/search_index.rs:850-872`: the preferred leaf is `<leaf>.ecsi`. The legacy
  leaf is `<leaf>` with its extension replaced by `.ecsi`. **The two name spaces overlap.** The
  preferred leaf of `foo.db3` (`foo.db3.ecsi`) is the legacy leaf of `foo.db3.bak`, and the
  preferred leaf of `foo` is the legacy leaf of `foo.db3`. So a file at the preferred name can
  belong to another database even when no swap happens.
* P7 — `src-tauri/src/db/search.rs:325-357` `open_valid_preferred`: the loader uses a preferred
  sidecar only when its full `IndexSource` equals the expected one, and treats `InvalidData` as
  "no index". A sidecar left behind for a database that no longer exists is never used, and the
  next index generation for whatever database later sits at that pathname replaces it.
* P8 — `src-tauri/src/infra/fs.rs:4781-4831` (unix `remove_entry_at`): `assert_entry_identity`
  (statat) is followed by `unlinkat`, with a `CommittedDurabilityUncertain` result if the parent
  sync fails. The Windows version (`:3637-3683`) opens with delete access, compares identity on
  that handle and unlinks through the handle.
* P9 — `src-tauri/src/db/mod.rs:3689` — the `db` test module is `#[cfg(all(test, unix))]`, so
  `unlink_database_files` tests run on Linux and macOS CI but never on Windows.
  `search_index.rs:1026-1027` tests are `#[cfg(test)]` and run on all three.
* P10 — the source scans in `src-tauri/src/infra/platform_support.rs:1628-1641`
  (`legacy_index_mapping_is_dropped_before_removal`, which scans `probe_legacy_index_sidecar_at`
  and `promote_legacy_index_sidecar_at`) and `:1726-1734` (Phase C row
  `"fn unlink_database_files("` must stay one ungated definition) pin text that this plan must
  keep true.

## Approach

### O1 — The preferred sidecar is removed only when it is not provably another database's

MANDATE words: "a valid index is destroyed for a database the user did not ask to touch";
"delete exactly the database the capability names".

* Required behavior. Before removing the preferred leaf, `unlink_database_files` takes its
  identity through `target.parent()`, opens it read-only through the same parent, checks that
  the handle has that identity, and reads the recorded `IndexSource` through O2. Then it closes
  the handle and decides:
  * The recorded source decodes and `recorded.object != expected_source.object` → **keep**. The
    file is not counted and no error results. This covers the finding's swap, where B's index
    records B's object, and the P6 name collision.
  * The recorded source decodes and `recorded.object == expected_source.object` → **remove**,
    whatever its revision, length or mtime. A stale index of this database is still this
    database's index.
  * The read reports `InvalidData` (wrong magic or version, a truncated or oversized source, an
    undecodable source) → **remove**. Such a file can be used by no loader (P7) and no promotion
    (P3 needs a valid current-version archive), so it is nobody's valid index. Today's behavior
    for this case is kept.
  * The file exists but this process may not read it: the identity probe or the read open fails
    with `io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied` after the existing `classify_probe_error` remapping,
    which still turns a Windows access-denied on a directory into `WrongKind`. → **keep**. The
    file is not counted, no error results, and the deletion continues. Its provenance cannot be
    read, so it may be another database's index, and removing it would be exactly the collateral
    the MANDATE forbids. Failing the deletion instead would block deleting the database over a
    file it cannot judge. On Windows the identity probe itself opens the leaf with read access
    (`infra/fs.rs:2738-2745`), so there the failure arrives at the probe. On unix it arrives at
    the read open. Both get the same disposition. (Added in r2 for I2; corrected in r3 from
    "remove" after the r2 closure check showed the removal could hit a foreign unreadable index.)
  * The removal is bound to the file whose provenance was judged. The identity is taken by
    `entry_identity_at` *before* the read open. If the opened handle's identity differs from it,
    the result is `Conflict`, because the file changed between the two calls. `remove_entry_at`
    is given that identity, so it refuses (`Conflict`) a file swapped in after the judgment. Such
    a conflict keeps today's path:
    through `remember_sidecar_error` it becomes an error returned before the primary is touched.
* Failure semantics for the identity probe, open or read. Every other error keeps today's
  classification of the `entry_identity_at` result, routed through `remember_sidecar_error` /
  `classify_probe_error`: `NotFound` → skip; `Reparse` / `WrongKind` →
  `InvalidInput("workspace sidecar must be a regular file")`; `MappedFile` / `Other` →
  propagate. Every sidecar failure before the primary still returns an error and never
  `PartialRemoval` (`d-20260831-24`).
* Order is unchanged, as `d-20260831-24` decided: preferred → legacy → primary → `-wal`/`-shm`.
* The existing test hook `UNLINK_SIDECAR_AFTER_IDENTITY_PROBE_HOOK` must fire after the
  provenance read and before the removal, for both sidecars, so that a test can swap the file in
  exactly the window O1 closes.
* Windows: the read handle is closed before `remove_entry_at` opens the leaf with delete access.
  This is the same sequence the legacy path uses.

### O2 — One header-only reader of an archive's recorded source

MANDATE words: "a sidecar identity/provenance check through the retained parent immediately
before each `unlinkat` (the legacy path already does `legacy_sidecar_matches`)".

* Required behavior. A `pub(crate)` function in `src-tauri/src/db/search_index.rs` takes an
  opened sidecar file and returns its recorded `IndexSource`. It reads only `HEADER_SIZE` bytes
  plus the declared source length (at most `MAX_SOURCE_BYTES`) into a buffer aligned for rkyv.
  It creates no memory mapping and reads no chunk. It returns `io::ErrorKind::InvalidData` for
  every header or source defect that `open_file_inner` rejects before its chunk loop, with the
  same messages.
* Sharing contract (rule 11, the second copy). The header and source checks (`verify_header`,
  the `MAX_SOURCE_BYTES` bound, alignment, the rkyv decode) live in one helper. Both
  `open_file_inner` and the new reader call it, so the two can never disagree about what counts
  as a decodable source.
* Second consumer. `legacy_sidecar_matches` in `db/mod.rs` switches from
  `probe_legacy_index_sidecar_at` to this reader. **Its predicate is unchanged**: keep unless the
  full recorded `IndexSource` equals `expected_source`. Its error classes are unchanged
  (`Malformed` / `NotFound` / `Reparse` / `WrongKind` → keep, `MappedFile` / `Other` →
  propagate) with one exception: a permission failure (`io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied`, after
  the existing remapping) → keep. That makes it the same disposition O1 gives the preferred
  sidecar, and the only one consistent with the legacy predicate itself ("keep unless provably
  this database's"). The legacy name is shared across databases (P6), so an unjudgeable legacy
  file may be another database's, and today's propagation blocks this database's deletion over
  it. Both deletion-time checks then use one mechanism and one classification of probe failures,
  and neither maps a file during deletion. Two behavior changes are named. A legacy file whose
  header and source match this database but whose chunks are corrupt is now removed instead of
  kept, because it is this database's file and useless to every loader. A legacy file this
  process may not read is now kept instead of failing the deletion. (The permission clause was
  added in r3 under I2: a same-area inconsistency that O1's corrected rule created, which
  PLAN-ONLY makes a plan obligation rather than a filing.)
* `probe_legacy_index_sidecar_at` keeps its full validation and remains the promotion probe
  (`promote_legacy_index_sidecar_at` copies the file, so it needs a valid archive). P10's scan
  of it stays true.
* Why not reuse `probe_legacy_index_sidecar_at` for the preferred sidecar: it maps and walks
  every chunk, so each deletion would cost time in proportion to the index size, and it would
  map a file that is about to be unlinked. Only the recorded source is needed.

### O3 — The remaining limits are written where the mechanism lives

* A doc comment on `unlink_database_files` states three things. Provenance and removal are
  bound to one judged file identity. The POSIX `statat`→`unlinkat` instant inside `remove_entry_at`
  remains (P8; it is shared with the primary and the legacy sidecar), and Windows has none. A
  sidecar this database wrote under a database object that has since changed is kept as
  foreign: that happens when the file's inode is replaced outside the app. P7 means it is never
  used and is overwritten by the next generation at that pathname. A sidecar this process may
  not read is kept for either name, because its provenance cannot be judged (r3, I2).

### O4 — Verification

All of these are Rust unit tests. Placement and platforms (changed in r2 for I3):

* Every `unlink_database_files` test below that needs no unix-only API goes in a `#[cfg(test)]`
  module of `db/mod.rs` that is **not** gated on `unix`. That covers items 1–5 and 9, including
  the moved existing test of item 5. Linux, macOS and Windows CI (`rust-windows-test`) all run
  them, so Windows executes the O1 open → close → identity-bound `remove_entry_at` sequence
  (P8's handle-based variant). The test-hook setter `set_unlink_sidecar_after_identity_probe_hook`
  goes from `#[cfg(all(test, unix))]` to `#[cfg(test)]` for that module. `for_test_path` and
  `IndexSource::from_database` are already `#[cfg(test)]` only. If a Windows fixture step cannot
  run as written (for example, renaming over a file whose handle the fixture still holds), the
  fixture is adapted and the assertion stays.
* Item 6 (a) needs `PermissionsExt`, so it stays in the `#[cfg(all(test, unix))]` module. Item 6
  (b) goes in the non-unix-gated module.
* The reader tests (item 7) go in `search_index.rs` and run on all three platforms.
* Windows proof level: `rust-windows-test` and `rust-macos-test` are CI-only (push skill §2). The
  push skill's post-push wait on those jobs for the pushed SHA is this plan's Windows and macOS
  proof, and a red job there returns to repair.

1. **The finding's scenario (regression anchor).** Database A has its own valid preferred index.
   Mint the `DatabaseFileTarget` and `expected_source` for A. Then rename database B over A's
   pathname and B's valid index, which records B's object, over the preferred leaf. Assert that
   `unlink_database_files` returns `Conflict("database changed before deletion")` and that B
   and B's index bytes are intact. With O1 reverted, B's index is gone and the test is red.
2. A stale preferred index of this database (A's object, different revision or length) is
   removed; the count is 2.
3. A malformed preferred file (non-archive bytes) is removed; the count is 2.
4. Foreign preferred, primary unchanged (P6): database `foo.db3` and a valid `foo.db3.ecsi`
   recording the object of a sibling `foo.db3.bak`. The primary is removed, the sidecar is
   kept, and the count is 1.
5. Swap between the provenance read and the unlink: the existing
   `unlink_database_files_rechecks_preferred_sidecar_identity_and_ignores_vanishing_sidecars`
   gets an A-owned valid archive as its original sidecar, so the "own" branch runs before the
   hook swaps the file. It still asserts `Conflict`, the replacement intact and the database
   intact.
6. Unreadable preferred sidecar (changed in r2, corrected in r3 for I2). (a) Unix fixture in the
   `#[cfg(all(test, unix))]` module: a valid archive recording another database's object, mode
   000, at the preferred name. The database is removed, the sidecar is kept with its bytes intact
   once permissions are restored, and the count is 1. (b) On every platform: the function that
   decides a sidecar's disposition from its probe result maps a constructed
   `Error::Io(io::Error::from(io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied))` to *keep* and a constructed
   `InvalidData` to *remove*. Windows denies access through ACLs, which this test suite cannot
   set, so (b) is how Windows CI runs the permission branch.
7. Reader: a valid archive returns its source. Bad magic, bad version, a truncated source and a
   `source_len` above `MAX_SOURCE_BYTES` return `InvalidData`. An archive with a valid header
   and source but a corrupt chunk payload returns its source from the reader while
   `MmapSearchIndex::open_file` rejects it. That last case proves the reader is header-only.
8. The existing deletion and promotion tests stay green unchanged except item 5:
   `delete_database_stops_at_invalid_preferred_sidecar_before_primary`,
   `delete_database_leaves_colliding_legacy_sidecar`,
   `unlink_database_files_skips_duplicate_legacy_leaf_on_extensionless_names`,
   `unlink_database_files_rejects_a_substituted_primary`,
   `unlink_database_files_rejects_an_identity_mismatch`,
   `unlink_database_files_skips_a_legacy_directory`,
   `legacy_sidecar_removal_uses_the_verified_identity_and_skips_corrupt_archives`, and the
   `platform_support.rs` scans from P10. The one existing test whose expectation changes on
   purpose is `legacy_sidecar_read_permission_failure_propagates`. It now asserts that a
   mode-000 legacy file is kept (`Ok(None)`) and that `unlink_database_files` still removes the
   database, and its name changes to say so (r3, I2).
9. **The legacy switch of O2, at deletion level (added in r2 for I1).** Database A's legacy
   sidecar is a file whose header and recorded source equal A's `expected_source` and whose
   chunk payload is corrupt. `unlink_database_files` removes it, and the count includes it.
   Under the old `probe_legacy_index_sidecar_at` path the full validation fails, the file is
   kept, and this test is red.

## Decisions and trade-offs

* **A provenance check, not a reorder.** Removing sidecars after the primary does not close the
  window. A swap that lands after the primary's unlink still puts B's index under the name about
  to be removed. Reordering would also reverse `d-20260831-24` without new evidence. The
  provenance check closes the probe-to-unlink window in either order, so the recorded order
  stays.
* **The preferred sidecar is compared on `object`, not on the full source.** Comparing the full
  `IndexSource` (the legacy rule) would leave every stale index of the deleted database on disk,
  because any write after indexing changes its revision, length or mtime. `object` is the part
  of the provenance that names *which* database.
* **An unreadable sidecar is kept and the deletion continues, for both names** (r3, I2). Today a
  unix deletion removes an unreadable preferred file, a Windows deletion fails on one, and an
  unreadable legacy file fails the deletion on both platforms. Keeping it is the only disposition
  that neither destroys a possibly foreign index nor blocks the deletion.
* **An undecodable preferred file is removed; an undecodable legacy file is kept.** The asymmetry
  is deliberate. Removing an undecodable file at the preferred name destroys no usable index,
  because no loader or promotion accepts one. Keeping it would leave a dead file behind every
  deletion after an `ECSI` version bump. The legacy rule stays as `d-20260831-24` set it.
* **A header-only reader** instead of the full-validation probe: see O2.

## Decided autonomously

* **Question:** What closes `f-20260912-09`'s window: removing sidecars after the primary, or a
  provenance check immediately before each sidecar unlink?
  **Chosen:** a provenance check. The preferred sidecar's recorded `IndexSource.object` is read
  through the retained parent with a header-only reader, and the removal is bound to the identity
  of the file that was read. The file is kept when it records another database's object, and
  removed when it records this database or is not a decodable current-version archive. The
  `d-20260831-24` order (preferred → legacy → primary → SQLite sidecars) is kept, and the legacy
  check moves onto the same reader with its predicate unchanged. A preferred file this process
  may not read is kept and the deletion continues, because its provenance cannot be judged.
  **Rejected:** removing sidecars after the primary, because a swap after the primary unlink
  still deletes the replacement's index and the reorder reverses `d-20260831-24` without new
  evidence. Also rejected: full `IndexSource` equality for the preferred sidecar, which would
  orphan every stale index of a deleted database; and reusing the full-validation
  `probe_legacy_index_sidecar_at`, whose cost grows with index size and which maps a file that
  is about to be deleted.
  **Because:** `IndexSource.object` is the provenance field that names the database, it is
  recorded in every archive (P4), and it is available before any chunk is read (P5).
  **Governs:** f-20260912-09.

## Risks / open questions

* A database whose inode was replaced outside the app keeps its old sidecar after deletion (O3).
  This is accepted: the loader never uses it (P7), and the next generation at that pathname
  overwrites it.
* The tests that stay in the pre-existing unix-only `db` module do not run on Windows (P9). That
  covers item 6 (a) and the existing tests listed in item 8. O4's placement moves this plan's
  deletion tests into a module Windows runs, but porting the whole `db` module is not this
  finding. (Wording corrected in r3 for I5.)
* An unreadable sidecar this database wrote stays after the deletion (I2). A later database at
  that pathname fails to load it with the same permission error the loader returns today for any
  unreadable sidecar (P7), until its permissions are restored.

## Not part of this task

* The `-wal`/`-shm` sidecar handling. Their identities are snapshotted and removed only after the
  primary has been verified (P2), so they are not exposed to this window.
* Primary-leaf deletion semantics, `PartialRemoval` reporting and registry cleanup
  (`d-20260831-24`, `f-20260917-08`).
* The mapping gate (`f-20260919-06`) and legacy promotion's own residual window (P3, the comment
  at `search_index.rs:914-919`).
* Porting the unix-only `db` test module to Windows.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — Provenance-checked sidecar removal

* Files: `src-tauri/src/db/search_index.rs` (O2 reader and shared parser, reader tests),
  `src-tauri/src/db/mod.rs` (O1, the O2 legacy switch, the O3 comment and the O4 tests).
* Subsystems: database deletion and search-index archive format. Touches persistence and
  concurrency semantics of a destructive command. No API or IPC contract changes, no bindings
  and no Specta types.
* Role: `sensitive` (`src-tauri/src/db/**` is a Sensitive-Path glob).
* PROOF:
  * `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked -- db::`
  * `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked -- infra::platform_support`
  * `cargo clippy --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings`
  * `cargo fmt --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml -- --check`
  * `pnpm checks:pre-review`
* Final gates: `pnpm gates:push -- --rust` (push skill §2), which also runs the backend
  coverage ratchet over the new branches and `rust:windows:check`. After the push, the push
  skill's wait on `rust-windows-test`, `rust-macos-test` and the `rust-platform` matrix for the
  pushed SHA is the runtime proof on Windows and macOS (O4 placement).

## Carried to diff review

* **CR-1** (I6, tests r2 late observation): O4 item 7's reader tests also cover a header with a
  valid, in-bounds, aligned `source_len` whose source bytes are not a decodable `IndexSource`.
  The reader returns `InvalidData` for it, and an `unlink_database_files` test with such a
  preferred file removes it (O1 `InvalidData` → remove). This is a test fixture inside unchanged
  obligations O2/O4. The diff review closes it.

### plan-r4.md

# Plan: database deletion removes only its own preferred search-index sidecar

## Goal

`unlink_database_files` must not destroy a valid search-index sidecar that belongs to another
database. Today it unlinks the preferred `.ecsi` sidecar before it learns whether the primary
leaf it is about to delete is still the database the caller resolved. The fix gives the preferred
sidecar the same kind of provenance check the legacy sidecar already has. The check reads the
sidecar's recorded database object through the retained parent, and the removal is bound to the
identity of that same opened file.

## MANDATE

Verbatim from `tasks/findings.md`, `f-20260912-09`:

> ### `unlink_database_files` removes the preferred sidecar before it checks the primary leaf, so a database replaced after the caller's probe loses the replacement's index
>
> * **Where:** `src-tauri/src/db/mod.rs:2257-2282` (`unlink_database_files`: `remove_optional_regular_at(preferred)`, then the legacy sidecar, then the primary's `statat` identity check, then `unlinkat`), the caller `delete_database_blocking` (`:2188-2215`, which probes through `database_identity_expected` first), and `d-20260831-24` (sidecar first, then legacy, then primary — the recorded order).
> * **Defect:** the caller's probe and the function's own primary check both happen at instants; between the probe and `remove_optional_regular_at`, or between a moved-up `statat` and that removal, an external rename can install database B and B's `.ecsi` at the same pathname. The preferred sidecar removed is then B's, and only afterwards does the primary check return `Conflict`. Sidecars are regenerable, so nothing is lost permanently, but a valid index is destroyed for a database the user did not ask to touch. Moving the `statat` before the sidecar removal narrows the window without closing it (measured in plan review, 2026-09-12: four lenses independently described the same post-check swap).
> * **Open question:** what closes the window — removing sidecars only after the primary has been unlinked (`d-20260831-24` chose sidecar-first so a failed primary unlink never leaves an orphaned index; the inverse leaves a sidecar whose provenance no longer matches, which the loader already rejects), or a sidecar identity/provenance check through the retained parent immediately before each `unlinkat` (the legacy path already does `legacy_sidecar_matches`)?
> * **Why it matters:** the deletion path's contract is "delete exactly the database the capability names"; an index for a different database is collateral.

## Threat model and non-goals

* **In scope: accidental concurrency.** Another program, such as a sync client, a backup restore
  or a file manager, renames a different database and its sidecar onto the same pathname
  while a deletion is running. The window that matters runs from the caller's identity probe
  through the exclusive-lock wait and the mapping-gate drain to the sidecar unlink. It can last
  as long as those waits take.
* **Out of scope: a deliberate adversary.** An adversary could race the single `statat`→`unlinkat`
  instant inside `remove_entry_at` on POSIX. POSIX has no pathname compare-and-unlink. The
  primary leaf and the legacy sidecar carry the same residual today, and
  `search_index.rs:914-919` already records it as a bounded known limit for legacy promotion.
  Windows deletes through the handle whose identity it checked
  (`infra/fs.rs:3637-3683`, `FILE_DISPOSITION_POSIX_SEMANTICS` per `d-20260919-10`), so Windows
  has no such residual.
* Frozen with the MANDATE.

## Traced premises

Read at `BASE` `e508f755`:

* P1 — `src-tauri/src/db/mod.rs:2665-2723` `delete_database_blocking`: `resolve_database(..,
  DatabaseMutate)` → `identity_from_probe` → `IndexSource::from_database_identity` → inside
  `delete_exclusive_cancellable`, `begin_preferred_replace` then `unlink_database_files(&target,
  &expected_source)`. `expected_source.object` is the probed database object.
* P2 — `src-tauri/src/db/mod.rs:2756-2925` `unlink_database_files` (the finding's line numbers
  have moved since it was filed). It snapshots the `-wal`/`-shm` identities, then runs
  `entry_identity_at(preferred)` followed by `remove_sidecar`. `remove_sidecar` calls
  `remove_entry_at(parent, leaf, identity, false)`. Then the legacy sidecar goes through
  `legacy_sidecar_matches`, then the primary goes through `remove_entry_at(target.identity())`,
  and a `Conflict` there becomes `Conflict("database changed before deletion")`. The SQLite
  sidecars are removed last. **The preferred sidecar's removal checks only that its
  `(dev, ino)` has not changed since a `statat` taken a moment earlier. Nothing ties that file
  to the database being deleted.**
* P3 — `src-tauri/src/db/mod.rs:2927-2949` `legacy_sidecar_matches` uses
  `search_index::probe_legacy_index_sidecar_at` (`search_index.rs:880-905`). That function
  opens the file through the retained parent, takes `opened_file_identity`, maps and fully
  validates the archive (`MmapSearchIndex::open_file`, which walks every chunk), compares the
  full `IndexSource`, and drops the mapping. A `Malformed`, `NotFound`, `Reparse` or `WrongKind`
  result means keep the file. `MappedFile` or `Other` propagates the error.
* P4 — `src-tauri/src/db/search_index.rs:165-171` `IndexSource { object, revision,
  database_length, database_modified_nanos }`. `object` is the database's `(dev, ino)` or, on
  Windows, its volume and file index. Every archive records the source it was built for.
* P5 — `src-tauri/src/db/search_index.rs:73-104` `verify_header` and `:620-645`
  (`open_file_inner`). The magic, version, `source_len` ≤ `MAX_SOURCE_BYTES` (1 MiB), alignment
  and rkyv decode of the source all run before any chunk is touched. `HEADER_SIZE` is 32 and
  `ARCHIVE_ALIGNMENT` is 16 (`:30-33`).
* P6 — `src-tauri/src/db/search_index.rs:850-872`: the preferred leaf is `<leaf>.ecsi`. The legacy
  leaf is `<leaf>` with its extension replaced by `.ecsi`. **The two name spaces overlap.** The
  preferred leaf of `foo.db3` (`foo.db3.ecsi`) is the legacy leaf of `foo.db3.bak`, and the
  preferred leaf of `foo` is the legacy leaf of `foo.db3`. So a file at the preferred name can
  belong to another database even when no swap happens.
* P7 — `src-tauri/src/db/search.rs:325-357` `open_valid_preferred`: the loader uses a preferred
  sidecar only when its full `IndexSource` equals the expected one, and treats `InvalidData` as
  "no index". A sidecar left behind for a database that no longer exists is never used, and the
  next index generation for whatever database later sits at that pathname replaces it.
* P8 — `src-tauri/src/infra/fs.rs:4781-4831` (unix `remove_entry_at`): `assert_entry_identity`
  (statat) is followed by `unlinkat`, with a `CommittedDurabilityUncertain` result if the parent
  sync fails. The Windows version (`:3637-3683`) opens with delete access, compares identity on
  that handle and unlinks through the handle.
* P9 — `src-tauri/src/db/mod.rs:3689` — the `db` test module is `#[cfg(all(test, unix))]`, so
  `unlink_database_files` tests run on Linux and macOS CI but never on Windows.
  `search_index.rs:1026-1027` tests are `#[cfg(test)]` and run on all three.
* P10 — the source scans in `src-tauri/src/infra/platform_support.rs:1628-1641`
  (`legacy_index_mapping_is_dropped_before_removal`, which scans `probe_legacy_index_sidecar_at`
  and `promote_legacy_index_sidecar_at`) and `:1726-1734` (Phase C row
  `"fn unlink_database_files("` must stay one ungated definition) pin text that this plan must
  keep true.

## Approach

### O1 — The preferred sidecar is removed only when it is not provably another database's

MANDATE words: "a valid index is destroyed for a database the user did not ask to touch";
"delete exactly the database the capability names".

* Required behavior. Before removing the preferred leaf, `unlink_database_files` takes its
  identity through `target.parent()`, opens it read-only through the same parent, checks that
  the handle has that identity, and reads the recorded `IndexSource` through O2. Then it closes
  the handle and decides:
  * The recorded source decodes and `recorded.object != expected_source.object` → **keep**. The
    file is not counted and no error results. This covers the finding's swap, where B's index
    records B's object, and the P6 name collision.
  * The recorded source decodes and `recorded.object == expected_source.object` → **remove**,
    whatever its revision, length or mtime. A stale index of this database is still this
    database's index.
  * The read reports `InvalidData` (wrong magic or version, a truncated or oversized source, an
    undecodable source) → **remove**. Such a file can be used by no loader (P7) and no promotion
    (P3 needs a valid current-version archive), so it is nobody's valid index. Today's behavior
    for this case is kept.
  * The file exists but this process may not read it → **keep**. This applies when any stage of
    the provenance probe fails with `io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied` after the existing
    `classify_probe_error` remapping (which still turns a Windows access-denied on a directory
    into `WrongKind`). The stages are: the identity probe (`entry_identity_at`), the read open,
    the opened handle's identity (`opened_file_identity`, which calls `file.metadata()` —
    `infra/path_authority/mod.rs:3434-3439`), and the header/source read. The file is not
    counted, no error results, and the deletion continues. Its provenance cannot be
    read, so it may be another database's index, and removing it would be exactly the collateral
    the MANDATE forbids. Failing the deletion instead would block deleting the database over a
    file it cannot judge. On Windows the identity probe itself opens the leaf with read access
    (`infra/fs.rs:2738-2745`), so there the failure arrives at the probe. On unix it arrives at
    the read open or the read. Every stage gets the same disposition. (Added in r2 for I2;
    corrected in r3 from "remove" after the r2 closure check showed the removal could hit a
    foreign unreadable index; extended in r4 to every probe stage.)
  * **One whole-probe error boundary (r4, I2).** For both sidecar names, every probe-stage result
    reaches one disposition decision. No stage keeps its own early-return path, including today's
    `Err(error) => remember_sidecar_error(...)?` after the preferred identity probe
    (`db/mod.rs:2855-2856`). The O2 reader preserves an I/O error's kind: a permission failure
    during the read stays `PermissionDenied`, and only truncation or format defects become
    `InvalidData`. Errors from the removal itself (`remove_entry_at`) are outside this exception
    and keep today's handling.
  * The removal is bound to the file whose provenance was judged. The identity is taken by
    `entry_identity_at` *before* the read open. If the opened handle's identity differs from it,
    the result is `Conflict`, because the file changed between the two calls. `remove_entry_at`
    is given that identity, so it refuses (`Conflict`) a file swapped in after the judgment. Such
    a conflict keeps today's path:
    through `remember_sidecar_error` it becomes an error returned before the primary is touched.
* Failure semantics for the identity probe, open or read. Every other error keeps today's
  classification of the `entry_identity_at` result, routed through `remember_sidecar_error` /
  `classify_probe_error`: `NotFound` → skip; `Reparse` / `WrongKind` →
  `InvalidInput("workspace sidecar must be a regular file")`; `MappedFile` / `Other` →
  propagate. Every sidecar failure before the primary still returns an error and never
  `PartialRemoval` (`d-20260831-24`).
* Order is unchanged, as `d-20260831-24` decided: preferred → legacy → primary → `-wal`/`-shm`.
* The existing test hook `UNLINK_SIDECAR_AFTER_IDENTITY_PROBE_HOOK` must fire after the
  provenance read and before the removal, for both sidecars, so that a test can swap the file in
  exactly the window O1 closes.
* Windows: the read handle is closed before `remove_entry_at` opens the leaf with delete access.
  This is the same sequence the legacy path uses.

### O2 — One header-only reader of an archive's recorded source

MANDATE words: "a sidecar identity/provenance check through the retained parent immediately
before each `unlinkat` (the legacy path already does `legacy_sidecar_matches`)".

* Required behavior. A `pub(crate)` function in `src-tauri/src/db/search_index.rs` takes an
  opened sidecar file and returns its recorded `IndexSource`. It reads only `HEADER_SIZE` bytes
  plus the declared source length (at most `MAX_SOURCE_BYTES`) into a buffer aligned for rkyv.
  It creates no memory mapping and reads no chunk. It returns `io::ErrorKind::InvalidData` for
  every header or source defect that `open_file_inner` rejects before its chunk loop, with the
  same messages, and a short read becomes `InvalidData` as truncation. Every other I/O error
  passes through with its kind, so a permission failure stays `PermissionDenied` (r4, I2).
* Sharing contract (rule 11, the second copy). The header and source checks (`verify_header`,
  the `MAX_SOURCE_BYTES` bound, alignment, the rkyv decode) live in one helper. Both
  `open_file_inner` and the new reader call it, so the two can never disagree about what counts
  as a decodable source.
* Second consumer. `legacy_sidecar_matches` in `db/mod.rs` switches from
  `probe_legacy_index_sidecar_at` to this reader. **Its predicate is unchanged**: keep unless the
  full recorded `IndexSource` equals `expected_source`. Its error classes are unchanged
  (`Malformed` / `NotFound` / `Reparse` / `WrongKind` → keep, `MappedFile` / `Other` →
  propagate) with one exception: a permission failure (`io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied`, after
  the existing remapping) at any stage of O1's whole-probe boundary → keep. That makes it the same disposition O1 gives the preferred
  sidecar, and the only one consistent with the legacy predicate itself ("keep unless provably
  this database's"). The legacy name is shared across databases (P6), so an unjudgeable legacy
  file may be another database's, and today's propagation blocks this database's deletion over
  it. Both deletion-time checks then use one mechanism and one classification of probe failures,
  and neither maps a file during deletion. Two behavior changes are named. A legacy file whose
  header and source match this database but whose chunks are corrupt is now removed instead of
  kept, because it is this database's file and useless to every loader. A legacy file this
  process may not read is now kept instead of failing the deletion. (The permission clause was
  added in r3 under I2: a same-area inconsistency that O1's corrected rule created, which
  PLAN-ONLY makes a plan obligation rather than a filing.)
* `probe_legacy_index_sidecar_at` keeps its full validation and remains the promotion probe
  (`promote_legacy_index_sidecar_at` copies the file, so it needs a valid archive). P10's scan
  of it stays true.
* Why not reuse `probe_legacy_index_sidecar_at` for the preferred sidecar: it maps and walks
  every chunk, so each deletion would cost time in proportion to the index size, and it would
  map a file that is about to be unlinked. Only the recorded source is needed.

### O3 — The remaining limits are written where the mechanism lives

* A doc comment on `unlink_database_files` states three things. Provenance and removal are
  bound to one judged file identity. The POSIX `statat`→`unlinkat` instant inside `remove_entry_at`
  remains (P8; it is shared with the primary and the legacy sidecar), and Windows has none. A
  sidecar this database wrote under a database object that has since changed is kept as
  foreign: that happens when the file's inode is replaced outside the app. P7 means it is never
  used and is overwritten by the next generation at that pathname. A sidecar this process may
  not read is kept for either name, because its provenance cannot be judged (r3, I2).

### O4 — Verification

All of these are Rust unit tests. Placement and platforms (changed in r2 for I3):

* Every `unlink_database_files` test below that needs no unix-only API goes in a `#[cfg(test)]`
  module of `db/mod.rs` that is **not** gated on `unix`. That covers items 1–5, 6 (b) and 9, including
  the moved existing test of item 5. Linux, macOS and Windows CI (`rust-windows-test`) all run
  them, so Windows executes the O1 open → close → identity-bound `remove_entry_at` sequence
  (P8's handle-based variant). The test-hook setter `set_unlink_sidecar_after_identity_probe_hook`
  goes from `#[cfg(all(test, unix))]` to `#[cfg(test)]` for that module. `for_test_path` and
  `IndexSource::from_database` are already `#[cfg(test)]` only. If a Windows fixture step cannot
  run as written (for example, renaming over a file whose handle the fixture still holds), the
  fixture is adapted and the assertion stays.
* Item 6 (a) needs `PermissionsExt`, so it stays in the `#[cfg(all(test, unix))]` module. Item 6
  (b) goes in the non-unix-gated module.
* The reader tests (item 7) go in `search_index.rs` and run on all three platforms.
* Windows proof level: `rust-windows-test` and `rust-macos-test` are CI-only (push skill §2). The
  push skill's post-push wait on those jobs for the pushed SHA is this plan's Windows and macOS
  proof, and a red job there returns to repair.

1. **The finding's scenario (regression anchor).** Database A has its own valid preferred index.
   Mint the `DatabaseFileTarget` and `expected_source` for A. Then rename database B over A's
   pathname and B's valid index, which records B's object, over the preferred leaf. Assert that
   `unlink_database_files` returns `Conflict("database changed before deletion")` and that B
   and B's index bytes are intact. With O1 reverted, B's index is gone and the test is red.
2. A stale preferred index of this database (A's object, different revision or length) is
   removed; the count is 2.
3. A malformed preferred file (non-archive bytes) is removed; the count is 2.
4. Foreign preferred, primary unchanged (P6): database `foo.db3` and a valid `foo.db3.ecsi`
   recording the object of a sibling `foo.db3.bak`. The primary is removed, the sidecar is
   kept, and the count is 1.
5. Swap between the provenance read and the unlink: the existing
   `unlink_database_files_rechecks_preferred_sidecar_identity_and_ignores_vanishing_sidecars`
   gets an A-owned valid archive as its original sidecar, so the "own" branch runs before the
   hook swaps the file. It still asserts `Conflict`, the replacement intact and the database
   intact.
6. Unreadable sidecar (changed in r2, corrected in r3, proof replaced in r4 for I2).
   (a) Real permission evidence, unix fixture in the `#[cfg(all(test, unix))]` module: a valid
   archive recording another database's object, mode 000, at the preferred name. The database is
   removed, the sidecar is kept with its bytes intact once permissions are restored, and the count
   is 1.
   (b) Deletion wiring on every platform, in the non-unix-gated module. A `#[cfg(test)]`
   thread-local fault injection sits at each probe stage's actual operation result, before any
   error translation: identity probe, read open, opened-handle identity, and header/source read,
   where the read fault enters before the reader translates errors. A scoped guard resets it.
   For each stage × each name (preferred, legacy), set up a database whose only sidecar is a
   valid archive whose recorded source **matches** this database, inject
   `io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied` at that stage, and call `unlink_database_files`. Assert that
   the fault was consumed, the result is `Ok((1, None))`, the primary is gone and the sidecar's
   bytes are unchanged. A stage that bypasses the fault removes the matching sidecar, and a stage
   that propagates the error fails the success assertion, so either is red. Windows denies access
   through ACLs, which this suite cannot set, so (b) is how Windows CI runs the permission
   branch.
7. Reader: a valid archive returns its source. Bad magic, bad version, a truncated source and a
   `source_len` above `MAX_SOURCE_BYTES` return `InvalidData`. An archive with a valid header
   and source but a corrupt chunk payload returns its source from the reader while
   `MmapSearchIndex::open_file` rejects it. That last case proves the reader is header-only.
8. The existing deletion and promotion tests stay green unchanged except item 5:
   `delete_database_stops_at_invalid_preferred_sidecar_before_primary`,
   `delete_database_leaves_colliding_legacy_sidecar`,
   `unlink_database_files_skips_duplicate_legacy_leaf_on_extensionless_names`,
   `unlink_database_files_rejects_a_substituted_primary`,
   `unlink_database_files_rejects_an_identity_mismatch`,
   `unlink_database_files_skips_a_legacy_directory`,
   `legacy_sidecar_removal_uses_the_verified_identity_and_skips_corrupt_archives`, and the
   `platform_support.rs` scans from P10. The one existing test whose expectation changes on
   purpose is `legacy_sidecar_read_permission_failure_propagates`. It now asserts that a
   mode-000 legacy file is kept (`Ok(None)`) and that `unlink_database_files` still removes the
   database, and its name changes to say so (r3, I2).
9. **The legacy switch of O2, at deletion level (added in r2 for I1).** Database A's legacy
   sidecar is a file whose header and recorded source equal A's `expected_source` and whose
   chunk payload is corrupt. `unlink_database_files` removes it, and the count includes it.
   Under the old `probe_legacy_index_sidecar_at` path the full validation fails, the file is
   kept, and this test is red.

## Decisions and trade-offs

* **A provenance check, not a reorder.** Removing sidecars after the primary does not close the
  window. A swap that lands after the primary's unlink still puts B's index under the name about
  to be removed. Reordering would also reverse `d-20260831-24` without new evidence. The
  provenance check closes the probe-to-unlink window in either order, so the recorded order
  stays.
* **The preferred sidecar is compared on `object`, not on the full source.** Comparing the full
  `IndexSource` (the legacy rule) would leave every stale index of the deleted database on disk,
  because any write after indexing changes its revision, length or mtime. `object` is the part
  of the provenance that names *which* database.
* **An unreadable sidecar is kept and the deletion continues, for both names** (r3, I2). Today a
  unix deletion removes an unreadable preferred file, a Windows deletion fails on one, and an
  unreadable legacy file fails the deletion on both platforms. Keeping it is the only disposition
  that neither destroys a possibly foreign index nor blocks the deletion.
* **An undecodable preferred file is removed; an undecodable legacy file is kept.** The asymmetry
  is deliberate. Removing an undecodable file at the preferred name destroys no usable index,
  because no loader or promotion accepts one. Keeping it would leave a dead file behind every
  deletion after an `ECSI` version bump. The legacy rule stays as `d-20260831-24` set it.
* **A header-only reader** instead of the full-validation probe: see O2.

## Decided autonomously

* **Question:** What closes `f-20260912-09`'s window: removing sidecars after the primary, or a
  provenance check immediately before each sidecar unlink?
  **Chosen:** a provenance check. The preferred sidecar's recorded `IndexSource.object` is read
  through the retained parent with a header-only reader, and the removal is bound to the identity
  of the file that was read. The file is kept when it records another database's object, and
  removed when it records this database or is not a decodable current-version archive. The
  `d-20260831-24` order (preferred → legacy → primary → SQLite sidecars) is kept, and the legacy
  check moves onto the same reader with its predicate unchanged. A preferred file this process
  may not read is kept and the deletion continues, because its provenance cannot be judged.
  **Rejected:** removing sidecars after the primary, because a swap after the primary unlink
  still deletes the replacement's index and the reorder reverses `d-20260831-24` without new
  evidence. Also rejected: full `IndexSource` equality for the preferred sidecar, which would
  orphan every stale index of a deleted database; and reusing the full-validation
  `probe_legacy_index_sidecar_at`, whose cost grows with index size and which maps a file that
  is about to be deleted.
  **Because:** `IndexSource.object` is the provenance field that names the database, it is
  recorded in every archive (P4), and it is available before any chunk is read (P5).
  **Governs:** f-20260912-09.

## Risks / open questions

* A database whose inode was replaced outside the app keeps its old sidecar after deletion (O3).
  This is accepted: the loader never uses it (P7), and the next generation at that pathname
  overwrites it.
* The tests that stay in the pre-existing unix-only `db` module do not run on Windows (P9). That
  covers item 6 (a) and the existing tests listed in item 8. O4's placement moves this plan's
  deletion tests into a module Windows runs, but porting the whole `db` module is not this
  finding. (Wording corrected in r3 for I5.)
* An unreadable sidecar this database wrote stays after the deletion (I2). A later database at
  that pathname fails to load it with the same permission error the loader returns today for any
  unreadable sidecar (P7), until its permissions are restored.

## Not part of this task

* The `-wal`/`-shm` sidecar handling. Their identities are snapshotted and removed only after the
  primary has been verified (P2), so they are not exposed to this window.
* Primary-leaf deletion semantics, `PartialRemoval` reporting and registry cleanup
  (`d-20260831-24`, `f-20260917-08`).
* The mapping gate (`f-20260919-06`) and legacy promotion's own residual window (P3, the comment
  at `search_index.rs:914-919`).
* Porting the unix-only `db` test module to Windows.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — Provenance-checked sidecar removal

* Files: `src-tauri/src/db/search_index.rs` (O2 reader and shared parser, reader tests),
  `src-tauri/src/db/mod.rs` (O1, the O2 legacy switch, the O3 comment and the O4 tests).
* Subsystems: database deletion and search-index archive format. Touches persistence and
  concurrency semantics of a destructive command. No API or IPC contract changes, no bindings
  and no Specta types.
* Role: `sensitive` (`src-tauri/src/db/**` is a Sensitive-Path glob).
* PROOF:
  * `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked -- db::`
  * `cargo test --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --locked -- infra::platform_support`
  * `cargo clippy --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings`
  * `cargo fmt --manifest-path src-tauri/Cargo.toml -- --check`
  * `pnpm checks:pre-review`
* Final gates: `pnpm gates:push -- --rust` (push skill §2), which also runs the backend
  coverage ratchet over the new branches and `rust:windows:check`. After the push, the push
  skill's wait on `rust-windows-test`, `rust-macos-test` and the `rust-platform` matrix for the
  pushed SHA is the runtime proof on Windows and macOS (O4 placement).

## Carried to diff review

  valid, in-bounds, aligned `source_len` whose source bytes are not a decodable `IndexSource`.
  The reader returns `InvalidData` for it, and an `unlink_database_files` test with such a

  obligations O2/O4. The diff review closes it.
